Another approach: Since objective is max of bilinear forms, we can use coordinate descent with exact pairwise updates. Let's explore. Pairwise update preserves sum. For a pair (i,j), set h_i = a + t, h_j = a - t, with sum constant; t interval ensuring [0,1]. F(t)=max_d T_d(t) where T_d is affine in t (because only products involving i or j: T_d = const + α_d t where α_d depends on neighbor values). So F is convex piecewise linear. We can find t minimizing F within interval by evaluating slope changes. But we can approximate with ternary/golden since convex piecewise linear, derivative monotone. Need subgradient at t. We can compute F and subgradient O(n) or O(active K). Performing many local pair updates may quickly drive to local optimum. We can random pairs, or all pairs sequentially.

Pairwise coordinate descent can be done at n maybe 200-500. Full sweep updating n pairs? If choose i randomly with j random, each eval O(K*n), maybe heavy. But we can update using current full objective array and adjust only affected lags incrementally to O(n) per update? Since changing two entries affects A_d and B_d for all d (pairs involving those indices). We can maintain A and B arrays and update in O(n) per coordinate pair (compute delta for each d). Then find t by scanning? Could be efficient.

Let's derive incremental updates for changing h_i, h_j. For each d, A_d =∑_{i=0}^{n-1-d} h_i(1-h_{i+d}). If h_i changes by δ_i, h_j by δ_j (sum zero), A_d changes only for terms where i or j appears:
- as left index p in [0,n-d-1]: delta_left(p,δ): (1-h_{p+d})δ - h_{p+d} δ? Actually T=(h_p + δ?)*(1-h_{p+d}) plus if same? If p changes, new-old = (h_p+δ)(1-h_{p+d}) - h_p(1-h_{p+d}) = δ(1-h_{p+d}). Wait product expansion: original h_p - h_ph_{p+d}; if only h_p changes and h_{p+d} fixed, delta=δ - h_{p+d}δ = δ(1-h_{p+d}). If h_{p+d} changes as right index q=p, delta = -h_q? More systematically, term h_i(1-h_j) with i=j+d? If left i changes: δ_i(1-h_j). If right j changes: -h_i δ_j. If both in same term (j=i+d or i=j+d): delta = (h_i+δ_i)(1-(h_j+δ_j)) - h_i(1-h_j)= δ_i - δ_i h_j - h_i δ_j + h_i h_j δ_j? If δ_i+δ_j? But if i and j are shifted by d, pair update affects same term bilinear.
For A_d, for each p where left p or right p+d equals i or j:
- i as left: p=i valid i<n-d: δ_i(1-h_{i+d}^{old?}) but if right also changes, need simultaneous. Simpler compute delta as new term - old term for the up to 4 affected terms per d? Each index appears in at most one A_d term as left (p=i) and one as right (p=i-d). For each d, affected if i in left set or right set. We can compute using old and new values for only affected p. For each d, wD = weight for A_d? scaled dx. g = ∑_d wA_d gA_d + ∑_d wB_d gB_d.

gA_d[p] = I_{p<n-d}(1-h[p+d]) - I_{p≥d}h[p-d]. Let's write for p:
Part A_linplus: ∑_{d: p < n-d} wA_d = ∑_{d=0}^{n-1-p} wA_d = prefixW_A[n-1-p]? since d max n-1-p.
Part A_neg1: -∑_{d: p < n-d} wA_d h[p+d] = -∑_{r=p+1}^{n-1} wA_{r-p} h[r] where d=r-p.
Part A_neg2: -∑_{d: p ≥ d} wA_d h[p-d] = -∑_{r=0}^{p} wA_{p-r} h[r].
So gA = prefixWA[n-1-p] - corr(wA, h)?? The two sums are convolution/correlation of wA with h. Let define for p:

Pairwise update (i,j), preserve sum:

Wait for fixed other variables, as function of a single coordinate x, each T_l is affine because product x*y with y fixed. For two coordinates with constraint sum, if they are paired in same term, term (h_i)(1-h_j) with h_i=a+t,h_j=b-t gives (a+t)(1-b+t)=ab? Actually product of (1 - h_j) with h_i: derivative second wrt t if i and j related: if h_i increases, h_j decreases, product (h_i)(1-h_j) = (a+t)(1-b+t) coefficient +1; term (h_j)(1-h_i) = (b-t)(1-a-t) coefficient +1; both convex. Fast objective implementation:
```
def erdos_terms(h):
    n=len(h)
    dx=2.0/n
    # autocorr
    N=1
    while N < 2*n: N*=2
    H=np.fft.rfft(h,N)
    ac=np.fft.irfft(H*np.conj(H),N)[:n]
    pref=np.concatenate(([0.0], np.cumsum(h)))
    total=pref[-1]
    d=np.arange(n)
    S1=pref[n-d]  # n-d for d 0..n-1 => n, n-1,...1
    S2=total - pref[d] # for d=0 total, d> prefix[d]
    A=S1-ac
    B=S2-ac
    return A,B,dx
```
Check S1 for d=n-1: pref[1] (h0), S2=total-pref[n-1] (last). Good.
Note total may be S plus tiny; for objective exact use total? Terms formula uses total. If sum not exact, okay.
Final exact c5: `max(np.correlate(h, 1-h, 'full')) * (2/n)`. Could call evaluate_eddos if available but compute ourselves. Need maybe use same formula? We'll compute exact and maybe if evaluate_erdos_solution available validate.

```
    if S==0: return zeros; if S==n: return ones

    hi=max(z)+1.0? Need tau such that h=clip(z-tau,0,1). If tau very low, all 1; if high all0. Use lo=min(z)-1-1e-12 gives h>=1? Actually z_i - (min-1) >=1 for min, so clip all at least1? Some >1 clip1 => sum n. hi=max(z)+1? z_i - max <=0 for max, maybe negative => all 0. Bisection.
```

Compute gradient:
Start `g = np.zeros(n)`. For each selected term:
- type A, d:
  if d==0: g += w*(1 - 2*h)
  else:
    # positive (p < n-d): 1 - h[p+d]
    idx = np.arange(n-d) # p
    g[:n-d] += w*(1 - h[d:])
    # negative (p >= d): -h[p-d]
    g[d:] += -w*h[:-d]
- type B,d:
  if d==0: same
  else:
    g[d:] += w*(1 - h[:-d])
    g[:n-d] += -w*h[d:]
Finally g *= dx.
This creates arrays; for K up to maybe 128 and n=256, 32k slice fills, ok. Use in-place scaled add. Avoid arange allocations? Use slices only.

Objective for line search: actual C5 using FFT terms (not np.correlate) for speed:
```
def fast_c5(h):
    A,B,dx=erdos_terms(h)
    return dx*np.max(np.maximum(A,B))
```
This should match exact within rounding. For final, use exact correlate.

```
def project_grad_step(h, eta, nu, K):
    vals... weights...
    g = grad_weights(...)*dx
    # if g norm ==0 return h
    # line search: try t = eta, then halve up to 10
    base_c = current actual max (we can pass)
    for attempt in range(12):
       cand=project_sum_box(h - t*g, int(n/2))
       c=fast_c5(cand)
       if c < base_c - 1e-12: return cand,c
       t*=0.5
    # if none, maybe accept small step or return h
    return h, base_c
```

Let's examine pairwise coordinate descent as polishing. It can directly reduce actual objective without smoothing. Could be robust and not require scipy. Let's design it in detail.

Pair update line search:
Given h feasible, choose i,j distinct. Let a=h[i], b=h[j]. Valid t range:
`t_low = max(-a, b-1)`, `t_high = min(1-a, b)`. If t_high-t_low < eps skip.
Define `set_pair(h, i,j,t)`: copy? For line search, we don't want allocate full copy each eval? Could update in place and revert. For evaluating F(t), need compute objective. We can create `h_try = h.copy(); h_try[i]=a+t; h_try[j]=b-t; project?` Since a+t,b-t within [0,1] and sum preserved, no projection needed. But numerical tiny, clip. Copy n each trial (n=256) small.
Line search for convex F(t): We can use golden-section or ternary with maybe 25 evaluations. But if many updates, heavy. Use `scipy.optimize.minimize_scalar`? Bounded, 1D, but overhead. We can implement efficient ternary:
```
lo=t_low; hi=t_high
for _ in range(24):
    m1=lo+(hi-lo)/3; m2=hi-(hi-lo)/3
    f1=fast_c5_with_copy(lo,m1); f2=...
    if f1 < f2: hi=m2 else lo=m1
 t=(lo+hi)/2
 if fast_c5(t) < c: accept
```
24+ evals per update. If full recompute FFT O(n log n) maybe 40 evals*? For 1000 updates=40k evals. n=256, FFT 512 maybe 0.0002? 8s; copy/cumsum Python maybe 0.0005 =>20s. Actually 40k*0.001=40s. Good. Could do several thousand. But choosing pairs random may not find global. Use local search with structured pairs.

Can improve by derivative-free "active pair selection": For each coordinate, identify direction to reduce active lags. But random may suffice with many iterations.

We can perform sweeps over adjacent pairs to smooth: for pass in range: for i in range(n): j=(i+1)%n? But updating overlapping pairs sequentially with exact sum. Adjacent pairs resembles local search; may reduce one-sided imbalances by redistributing mass. Since parent step function, adjacent pairs can shift boundaries.

```
def make_exact_sum(h, S):
    h=np.clip(h,0,1)
    diff=S-h.sum()
    if abs(diff)<1e-12: return h
    # find index not at bound, add diff
    for i in np.argsort(h): if 0<h[i]<1: h[i]+=diff; break
    # if not, adjust several
```

```
def improve_pg(h, max_time, nu0=0.05, K=None, initial_step=0.05, top_fraction=0.05):
    h = project_sum_box(np.asarray(h,float), int(len(h)/2))
    n = len(h); S = n//2
    c = fast_c5(h)
    step = 0.1
    nu = nu0
    adapt=0
    while time.time() < start? (pass deadline):
       # recompute values and weights
       vals, A, B, dx = term_values(h)
       maxval = max(A[0], A[1:],B[1:]).max()
       if maxval*dx < 1e-12: break
       # choose active terms
       vals = ... array
       # include maybe all if len<=K
       if K is None: K=max(16, min(256, int(0.15*(2*n-1))))
       if len(vals) <= K: sel=np.arange(len(vals)) else sel=np.argpartition(vals,-K)[-K:]
       # We need include ties near max: maybe extend if vals[sel].min() > maxval - nu*10? But argpartition K largest fine.
       # To avoid all weight on one term when K small, softmax.
       sv=vals[sel]; m=sv.max(); w=np.exp((sv-m)/nu); w/=w.sum()
       wA=np.zeros(n); wB=np.zeros(n)
       # map sel to type/d. Need vals structure. If vals concatenation [A0, A1..A[n-1], B1..B[n-1]], then:
       # index 0 => A d=0
       # index 1 + d => A d for d=1..n-1
```

```
for d, ww in enumerate(wA):
  if ww==0: continue
  if d==0: g += ww*(1-2*h)
  else:
     mp = n-d
     g[:mp] += ww*(1 - h[d:])

for d, ww in enumerate(wB):
  if ww==0 or d==0: continue
  g[d:] += ww*(1 - h[:-d])

g *= dx
```

Let's define `fast_c5` optimized:
```
def fast_c5(h):
    n=h.size
    dx=2.0/n
    N=1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()  # careful (2*n-1) int
    H=np.fft.rfft(h, N)
    ac=np.fft.irfft(H*np.conj(H), N)[:n]
    pref=np.empty(n+1); pref[0]=0; pref[1:]=np.cumsum(h)
    total=pref[-1]
    d=np.arange(n)
    S1=pref[n-d]
    S2=total - pref[d]
    A=S1-ac; B=S2-ac
    m = A[0]
    if n>1: m = max(m, A[1:].max(), B[1:].max())
    return m*dx
```
But allocate d each call; can precompute per n in a helper? Since many evals, we can create caches. But no closures/lambdas; can use global dictionaries? Top-level. However run function should not rely on persistent global? It can. But to be safe, inside run define local cache dict? That's not closure? local variables okay. The rule: no closures or lambdas; helper functions top-level. A local dict passed? We can have top-level global `fft_cache = {}` but in code block. Or simpler allocate d each call; n=256, negligible. arange cost small. Cumsum and FFT dominate.

```
_TERM_CACHE = {}
def get_term_cache(n):
    d = _TERM_CACHE.get(n)
    if d is None:
       N = 1
       while N < 2*n: N <<= 1
       d = {'N':N, 'd':np.arange(n), 'idx_n_minus_d':np.arange(n-1,-1,-1)?, 'idx_d':np.arange(n)}
       _TERM_CACHE[n]=d
    return d
```
`compute_terms`:
```
def compute_terms(h):
    n=h.size; c=get_term_cache(n)
    H=np.fft.rfft(h,c['N'])
    ac=np.fft.irfft(H*np.conj(H), c['N'])[:n]
    pref=np.concatenate(([0.0], np.cumsum(h)))
    total=pref[-1]
    A = pref[n - c['d']] - ac
    B = total - pref[c['d']] - ac
    return A,B,2.0/n
```
For d array, `n - d` gives int array [n, n-1, ...,1]. Good indexing pref (length n+1). d=0 => n; d=n-1 =>1. `pref[d]` length n. Good.

But `np.concatenate` each call. Use preallocated? Could:
```
pref = np.empty(n+1); pref[0]=0; np.cumsum(h, out=pref[1:])
```
But `np.cumsum` out requires dtype; okay. Allocate pref each call; n small. Could store pref in cache? Can't because h changes. Use local.

Exact final:
```
def exact_c5(h):
    n=len(h); dx=2.0/n
    corr=np.correlate(h, 1.0-h, mode='full')
    return float(np.max(corr)*dx)
```
Could corr have tiny negative? h,b nonnegative, so no. Use max.

Projection:
```
def project_sum_box(z, S):
    n=len(z)
    if S <= 0: return np.zeros(n)
    if S >= n: return np.ones(n)
    # initialize tau? Use min/max
    lo = float(np.min(z)) - 1.0
    hi = float(np.max(z)) + 1.0  # at hi, z_i-hi <=0? for max yes; for others maybe negative; sum maybe not 0 if some z_i > hi? hi=max+1 so z_i - hi <0 for all, clip 0. Sum 0. At lo=min-1, min_i - lo=1, others >=, clip1 => sum n.
    for _ in range(80):
       tau=(lo+hi)/2
       h=np.clip(z - tau, 0.0, 1.0)
       s=h.sum()
       if s > S: lo=tau
       else: hi=tau
    h=np.clip(z - (lo+hi)/2,0,1)
    # final correction: residual maybe due bisection; adjust non-bound
    err=float(S-h.sum())
    if abs(err) < 1e-12: return h
    # find indices with 0<h<1
    idx=np.flatnonzero((h>1e-9)&(h<1-1e-9))
    if idx.size:
       h[idx[0]] += err/idx.size? Need keep bounds.
       # distribute over all nonbounds
       per=err/idx.size
       if np.all((h[idx]+per)>=0) and ...: h[idx]+=per
       else: h[idx[0]] += err
    # if still out, fallback: h = np.clip(h,0,1); adjust one
    h=np.clip(h,0,1)
    err=S-h.sum()
    if abs(err)>1e-12:
       idx=np.flatnonzero((h>1e-8)&(h<1-1e-8))
       if idx.size: h[idx[0]] += err
       else:
          # rare: all 0/1 but sum wrong; adjust a 0 if err>0 or 1 if err<0
          if err>0: idx=np.flatnonzero(h<1); h[idx[0]]=1.0
          else: idx=np.flatnonzero(h>0); h[idx[0]]=0.0
    return h
```
Bisection 80 may be overkill; 50 enough. But projection called many times. Optimize: If z already in [0,1] and sum close, we can just adjust? For PG line search, z=h - t*dir may be outside box a little. Bisection 50* n operations. If n=512, 25k per projection, thousands =>25M okay. Can reduce to 40. Use 60 for safety.

Alternatively, if z mostly in bounds, water-filling could be faster, but okay.

Potential issue: projection onto sum with box for S integer. The bisection finds tau where sum crosses; because clip function flat, sum may not be continuous? It is continuous and piecewise linear, bisection okay. If S equals plateau, many tau; fine.

Upsampling:
```
def resample_h(h0, n1):
    n0=len(h0)
    h0=np.asarray(h0,float)
    if n0==n1: return project_sum_box(h0, n1//2)
    x0=(np.arange(n0)+0.5)*2.0/n0
    x1=(np.arange(n1)+0.5)*2.0/n1
    h1=np.interp(x1, x0, h0, left=h0[0], right=h0[-1])
    return project_sum_box(h1, n1//2)
```
Maybe linearly interpolate can create sum not exact; project. Good.

```
def gaussian_smooth(h, sigma):
```

Let's design `pair_minimize(h,i,j,c_current, max_evals=12, min_improve=1e-12)`:
```
def pair_minimize(h, i, j, c_current, max_evals=12):
    n=len(h); a=h[i]; b=h[j]
    lo=max(-a, b-1.0)
    hi=min(1.0-a, b)
    if hi-lo < 1e-10: return h,c_current,i,j,0
    # ensure t=0 within [lo,hi]; due floats yes.
    # If t=0 at boundary? a=0 or b=1? still hi/lo.
    phi=(1+sqrt(5))/2; gr=phi-1
    x1=lo+(hi-lo)*gr? Golden: c1=lo+(hi-lo)/phi, c2=hi-(hi-lo)/phi.
    f1=eval_x(x1); f2=eval_x(x2); best_t=... best_f=min(f1,f2,c_current?)
    for k in range(max_evals-2):
       if f1 < f2: hi=c2; c2=c1; f2=f1; c1=lo+(hi-lo)/phi; f1=eval(c1)
       else: lo=c1; c1=c2; f1=f2; c2=hi-(hi-lo)/phi; f2=eval(c2)
       update best
    # check t=0? Already c_current might be best. Maybe evaluate midpoint final
    t=0.5*(lo+hi)
    ft=eval(t); update best
    # Check endpoints? lo/hi maybe lo=0, hi maybe boundary. If lo>0? t=0 not endpoint if bounds due a,b? lo may >0 if b<1? Let's see lo=max(-a,b-1). Since a,b in [0,1], -a≤0, b-1≤0, so lo≤0. hi≥0. t=0 always inside or boundary? if a=0, lo= -0 or b-1≤0 => lo=0? if b=1, hi=0? yes. So t=0 boundary possible. hi could be boundary. We know f(0)=c_current. Min at hi; golden may evaluate near.
    if best_f < c_current - min_improve and best_t !=0:
       h[i]=a+best_t; h[j]=b-best_t; return h,best_f,...
    else no change
```
Need store old i/j values if best not accepted; after evals we restored each eval. At end if accept, set.
In eval, ensure t within [lo,hi] and values within [0,1] (clip tiny).
```
def eval_t(h,i,j,a,b,t):
   x=a+t; y=b-t
   if x<0: x=0; if x>1:x=1; if y<0:y=0; if y>1:y=1
   h[i]=x; h[j]=y; c=fast_c5(h); h[i]=a; h[j]=b; return c
```
But if t near boundary, clipping might make sum not exact (if x clipped). But t inside valid so no clip except eps. Fine.

Pseudo:
```
def improve_pairs(h, max_time, rng, max_evals=10, gradient=True):
    n=len(h); S=n//2
    h=project_sum_box(h,S)
    c=fast_c5(h)
    deadline=time.time()+max_time-10
    fail=0
    iter_count=0
    g=None
    while time.time()<deadline:
        iter_count +=1
        if gradient and (g is None or iter_count % 3 ==0):
            g = compute_gradient_current(h, nu=0.02, K=16)  # raw maybe
            c? g may be None
        # choose pair
        if rng.random() < 0.7 and g is not None:
            gm = g - g.mean()
            pos = gm > 1e-8; neg = gm < -1e-8
            # donor high g, receiver low g. Move from donor to receiver: h_donor - t, h_receiver + t if gm_donor>gm_receiver.
            pos_candidates = np.where(pos & (h>1e-8))[0]
            neg_candidates = np.where(neg & (h<1-1e-8))[0]
            if pos_candidates.size and neg_candidates.size:
                weights = np.maximum(gm,0); i = choice pos weighted
                weig = np.maximum(-gm,0); j = choice neg weighted
                ensure i!=j; if same random
            else random distinct
        else:
            i,j=random pair
        # optional: if gm_i < gm_j, swap so donor high
        h,c2 = pair_minimize(...)
        if c2 < c - 1e-11:
            c=c2; fail=0
        else:
            fail+=1
            if fail > 50:
                # try random small perturb many
                for _ in range(10): random pair with random t evaluate; accept if lower
                fail=0
                g=None
        # if c lower than local best? keep
        # limit time
    return project_sum_box(h,S), c
```

Let's examine possible failure of pair line search due to objective nonconvex? We claim F(t) convex. Let's verify all terms are convex in t when only i,j change with sum zero. For a term T = sum over edges (maybe includes h_i and h_j). Cases:
- Edge not involving i/j: constant.
- Edge involving one variable (left i, right fixed): affine in t (coefficient ±).
- Edge involving both variables: If edge is (i,j) with i left j right: (a+t)(1-(b-t)) = (a+t)(1-b+t) = quadratic t^2 + (a+1-b)t + a(1-b). coefficient +1.
If edge is (j,i) for another orientation: (b-t)(1-(a+t)) = (b-t)(1-a-t) = t^2 - (b+1-a?)t + ... coefficient +1.
What about edge where both variables appear in linear sum separate? T expanded includes linear h_i and -h_i h_j; if i,j both in product, quadratic +1, linear includes t terms. All coefficients +1 for product terms when opposite movement. Could a term contain product h_i*h_j but with one variable sign leading coefficient negative? Original T_d^A = ∑ h_left - h_left h_right. If left=i, right=j: coefficient from -h_i h_j with h_i=a+t,h_j=b-t => -[(a+t)(b-t)] = -[ab + (a-b)t - t^2] = -ab - (a-b)t + t^2. coefficient +1. If linear + from h_i gives +t. Total coefficient t^2 + positive. For B similar. If an edge involves i as left and j as right in A, as above. If i as right, j as left? That product coefficient also +1 after negative. Thus each term convex quadratic (or affine). Max convex => convex. Good.

```
def active_gradient(h, nu, K, rng=None, random_single_prob=0.15):
    A,B,dx=compute_terms(h)
    n=len(h)
    vals=np.empty(2*n-1); vals[:n]=A; vals[n:]=B[1:]
    L=vals.size
    if K is None: K=max(8, min(64, L//4))
    K=min(K,L)
    sel = np.argsort(vals)[-K:] if K<L else np.arange(L)  # sorting 1000 fine
    sv=vals[sel]
    m=sv.max()
    w=np.exp((sv-m)/nu); w/=w.sum()
    if rng is not None and rng.random() < random_single_prob:
        # choose one random among top K with softmax weights (rmultinomial)
        idx=np.random.default? Use rng.choice(K,p=w)
        w = np.zeros(K); w[idx]=1.0
    else:
        # add tiny random tie-breaker
        if rng is not None and rng.random() < 0.3:
           w = w * rng.uniform(0.5,1.5,K); w/=w.sum()
    # assign
    wA=np.zeros(n); wB=np.zeros(n)
    for idx,ww in zip(sel,w):
       if idx<n: wA[idx]=ww
       else: wB[idx-n+1]=ww
    g = gradient_from_weights(h,wA,wB,dx)
    c=fast_c5_from_terms(A,B,dx)
    return g,c,vals
```
Sorting every iteration okay. Could use `np.argpartition` for speed but need order for softmax not. Sorting 1000*1000=1M log small. Use argsort simple.

### Cache and terms

```
_TERM_CACHE = {}

def _term_cache(n):
    c = _TERM_CACHE.get(n)
    if c is None:
        N = 1
        need = 2*n - 1
        while N < need:
            N <<= 1
        c = {'N': N, 'd': np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64), 'n_minus_d': np.arange(n, 0, -1, dtype=np.int64)}
        _TERM_CACHE[n] = c
    return c
```
`n_minus_d` length n: [n, n-1,...,1]. Good.

```
def compute_terms(h):
    n = h.size
    cache = _term_cache(n)
    H = np.fft.rfft(h, cache['N'])
    ac = np.fft.irfft(H * np.conj(H), cache['N'])[:n]
    pref = np.empty(n+1, dtype=float)
    pref[0]=0.0
    np.cumsum(h, out=pref[1:], dtype=float) # np.cumsum has dtype? In recent numpy, cumsum(out=, dtype=) okay? To be safe: pref[1:] = np.cumsum(h, dtype=float)
    total = pref[-1]
    d = cache['d']
    index_n_minus = cache['n_minus_d']
    A = pref[index_n_minus] - ac
    B = total - pref[d] - ac
    dx = 2.0 / n
    return A, B, dx
```
`np.cumsum(h, dtype=float)` returns new; okay. Assignment.

`fast_c5_from_terms(A,B,dx)`:
```
def fast_c5_from_terms(A,B,dx):
    m = float(A[0])
    if A.size > 1:
        ma = float(np.max(A[1:]) )
        if ma > m: m=ma
        mb = float(np.max(B[1:]))
        if mb > m: m=mb
    return m*dx
```
For n=1? Not use.

`fast_c5(h)`:
```
def fast_c5(h):
    A,B,dx=compute_terms(h)
    return fast_c5_from_terms(A,B,dx)
```

`exact_c5(h)`:
```
def exact_c5(h):
    n=h.size
    corr=np.correlate(h, 1.0-h, mode='full')
    return float(np.max(corr) * (2.0/n))
```
If n=0? no.

### Projection

```
def project_sum_box(z, S):
    n=z.size
    if n == 0: return z.copy()
    if S <= 0: return np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    if S >= n: return np.ones(n, dtype=float)
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=float)
    # If already close in sum and bounds, quick fix?
    h = np.clip(z, 0.0, 1.0)
    s = float(h.sum())
    if abs(s - S) < 1e-12:
        return h
    lo = float(np.min(z)) - 1.0
    hi = float(np.max(z)) + 1.0
    # If z all within [0,1] and sum close, could distribute? Bisection okay.
    for _ in range(60):
        tau = 0.5*(lo+hi)
        h = np.clip(z - tau, 0.0, 1.0)
        s = float(h.sum())
        if s > S:
            lo = tau
        else:
            hi = tau
    h = np.clip(z - 0.5*(lo+hi), 0.0, 1.0)
    # correct residual
    err = float(S - h.sum())
    if err != 0.0:
        if abs(err) < 1e-10:
            # distribute among non-boundaries
            idx = np.where((h > 1e-9) & (h < 1.0 - 1e-9))[0]
            if idx.size:
                h[idx] += err / idx.size
            else:
                h[0] += err
        else:
            # grow/shrink from ends? If many bounds, adjust a set.
            if err > 0:
                order = np.argsort(h)  # smallest
                for idx in order:
                    cap = 1.0 - h[idx]
                    add = min(cap, err)
                    h[idx] += add
                    err -= add
                    if err <= 1e-14: break
            else:
                order = np.argsort(-h)
                for idx in order:
                    sub = min(h[idx], -err)
                    h[idx] -= sub
                    err += sub
                    if abs(err) <= 1e-14: break
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    # final tiny correction
    err = float(S - h.sum())
    if abs(err) > 1e-13:
        idx = np.where((h > 1e-9) & (h < 1.0 - 1e-9))[0]
        if idx.size:
            h[idx[0]] += err
        else:
            h = np.clip(h, 0, 1)
            if err > 0:
                idx = np.where(h < 1.0)[0]
                if idx.size: h[idx[0]] = 1.0
            else:
                idx = np.where(h > 0.0)[0]
                if idx.size: h[idx[0]] = 0.0
    return h
```
This is robust but maybe heavy. Could optimize: In projection, z from `h - t*dir` may be slightly out; bisection 60 * each line search. To reduce, use 45. 60 okay? If 10k projections*60*512=307M operations maybe too high. Need optimize projection.

We can implement a faster projection using water-filling only when bounds not clipped much. Since target S integer and h already feasible, gradient step small; z is near [0,1]. We can project by simply clipping and then adjusting sum over non-bound clips. Most z within [0,1] if step small. Cases:
- If min(z)>=0 and max(z)<=1: then find tau=(sum(z)-S)/n, set h=z-tau. Since no clipping needed if tau small such that bounds not violated. But tau may push some out. Check after: if all h in [0,1], done. This O(n) no bisection.
- If some z below 0 or above 1, solve with bisection.
So:
```
z = np.asarray(z,float)
s0=z.sum()
if min(z)>=0 and max(z)<=1:
   tau=(s0-S)/n
   h=z-tau
   if h.min()>=-1e-15 and h.max()<=1+1e-15:
       h=np.clip(h,0,1); fix tiny; return
# else bisection
```

Quick fix function similar but simpler:
```
def quick_feasible(z,S):
    h=z.astype(float, copy=True)
    h -= (h.sum()-S)/h.size
    h = np.clip(h,0,1)
    err = S - h.sum()
    if abs(err) > 1e-12:
       idx = np.where((h>1e-9)&(h<1-1e-9))[0]
       if idx.size: h[idx] += err/idx.size
       else:
       ... adjust sequential
    h = np.clip(h,0,1)
    return h
```
Should be feasible within 1e-12.

`improve_pg` implementation with quick_feasible:
```
def improve_pg(h, max_time, rng=None, nu=0.05, K=None, initial_step=0.08, deadline=None):
    n=h.size; S=n//2
    h=project_sum_box(h,S)
    c=fast_c5(h)
    if deadline is None: deadline=time.time()+max_time-5
    step=initial_step
    stagnant=0
    iter_count=0
    while time.time() < deadline:
        iter_count +=1
        g,c2,A,B,vals,sel? = active_gradient(...)
        if c2 < c: c=c2? active_gradient computed at h, should equal c but due recompute maybe.
```

Gradient from weights:
```
def gradient_from_weights(h, wA, wB, dx):
    g=np.zeros_like(h)
    nzA=np.nonzero(wA)[0]
    for d in nzA:
       ww=float(wA[d])
       if ww==0: continue
       if d==0:
          g += ww*(1.0 - 2.0*h)
       else:
          mp=n-d
          g[:mp] += ww*(1.0 - h[d:])
          g[d:] -= ww*h[:-d]
    nzB=np.nonzero(wB)[0]
    for d in nzB:
       ww=float(wB[d])
       if d==0: continue
       g[d:] += ww*(1.0 - h[:-d])
       g[:n-d] -= ww*h[d:]
    g *= dx
    return g
```
Need pass n or use h.size. Slices.

Potential performance: `np.nonzero(wA)` each gradient O(n), okay.

```
def _pair_eval(h,i,j,a,b,t):
    x=a+t; y=b-t
    if x < 0.0: x = 0.0
    elif x > 1.0: x = 1.0
    if y < 0.0: y=0.0
    elif y>1.0: y=1.0
```

```
def improve_pairs(h, max_time, rng, search_evals=8, deadline=None):
    n=h.size; S=n//2
    h=project_sum_box(h,S)
    c=fast_c5(h)
    if deadline None: deadline=time.time()+max_time-5
    pass_count=0
    no_improve=0
    while time.time() < deadline:
        mode = modes[pass_count % len(modes)]
        if mode == 'random': order = list(range(n)); rng.shuffle(order)
        else: order = list(range(n))
        for i in order:
            if mode == 'adjacent': j=(i+1)%n
            elif mode == 'opposite': j=(i+n//2)%n; if j==i: j=(i+1)%n
            else:
               j = int(rng.integers(n))
               if j==i: j=(i+1)%n
            h2,c2=pair_minimize(h,i,j,c,search_evals)
            if c2 < c - 1e-12:
                c=c2; no_improve=0
            else:
                no_improve +=1
            if time.time() > deadline - 30: break? We can break loops to finish.
        pass_count +=1
        if no_improve > n*3:
            # maybe random mode more
            # pass_count already cycles; set modes? 
            no_improve=0
    h=project_sum_box(h,S)
    return h,c
```

```
def smooth_h(h, sigma):
    if sigma <=0: return h.copy()
    r = int(np.ceil(3*sigma)) if sigma>0 else 0
    if r ==0: return h.copy()
    k = np.exp(-0.5*(np.arange(-r,r+1)/sigma)**2)
    k /= k.sum()
    # pad with edge values to avoid edge dip
    hp = np.pad(h, r, mode='edge')
```

```
def smooth_h(h, sigma):
    r=int(math.ceil(3.0*sigma))
    if r<=0: return h.copy()
    x=np.arange(-r,r+1,dtype=float)
    k=np.exp(-0.5*(x/sigma)**2); k/=k.sum()
    hp=np.pad(h, r, mode='edge')
    hs=np.convolve(hp, k, mode='same')[:len(h)]
    return hs
```

Implement `active_gradient`:
```
def active_gradient(h, nu=0.02, K=None, rng=None, random_single_prob=0.15):
    A,B,dx = compute_terms(h)
    c = fast_c5_from_terms(A,B,dx)
    n = A.size
    L = 2*n -1
    vals = np.empty(L)
    vals[:n] = A
    vals[n:] = B[1:]
    if K is None:
        K = max(8, min(128, (L // 4) + 1))
    K = int(min(K, L))
    if K < L:
        sel = np.argpartition(vals, -K)[-K:]
    else:
        sel = np.arange(L)
    sv = vals[sel]
    m = sv.max()
    # if m==0? weights uniform? exp handles
    w = np.exp((sv - m) / max(nu,1e-8))
    w /= w.sum()
    if rng is not None:
        if random_single_prob > 0.0 and rng.random() < random_single_prob:
            idx = int(rng.choice(K, p=w))
            w_new = np.zeros(K); w_new[idx]=1.0
        else:
            w_new = w
            if rng.random() < 0.3:
                mult = rng.uniform(0.5, 1.5, K)
                w_new = w_new * mult
                w_new /= w_new.sum()
    else: w_new=w
    wA = np.zeros(n); wB = np.zeros(n)
    for idx, ww in zip(sel, w_new):
        idx=int(idx)
        if idx < n:
            wA[idx] = ww
        else:
            wB[idx - n + 1] = ww
    g = gradient_from_weights(h, wA, wB, dx)
    return g, c, vals
```
Need ensure `w_new` assigned if random single but `w` maybe used; okay.
If `rng.choice(K,p=w)` with K maybe np.int; ok. If K=1, works.

Quick_feasible definition:
```
def quick_feasible(z, S):
    h = np.array(z, dtype=float, copy=True)
    h -= (h.sum() - S) / h.size
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    err = float(S - h.sum())
    if abs(err) > 1e-12:
        idx = np.where((h > 1e-9) & (h < 1.0 - 1e-9))[0]
        if idx.size:
            h[idx] += err / idx.size
        else:
            # sequential
            if err > 0:
               order = np.argsort(h)
            else: order=np.argsort(-h)
            for idx2 in order:
                if err > 0:
                   add = min(1.0 - h[idx2], err)
                   h[idx2] += add; err -= add
                else:
                   sub = min(h[idx2], -err)
                   h[idx2] -= sub; err += sub
                if abs(err) <= 1e-14: break
    h = np.clip(h,0,1)
    return h
```
If err large due z out of bounds, distributing equally over nonbounds may push out; sequential safer. But in PG z mostly feasible. Good.

Implement `resample_h(h0, n1, mode='linear')`:
```
def resample_h(h0, n1, mode='linear'):
    n0=h0.size
    if n0 == n1: return np.array(h0, copy=True)
    if mode == 'nearest':
       idx = ((np.arange(n1)+0.5) * n0 / n1).astype(int)  # since x_new/dx_new? Actually centers scaled: x_new center = (i+0.5)/n1 fraction; index floor((i+0.5)*n0/n1)
       idx = np.clip(idx,0,n0-1)
       return h0[idx].copy()
    else:
       x0=(np.arange(n0)+0.5)/n0
       x1=(np.arange(n1)+0.5)/n1
       return np.interp(x1, x0, h0)
```

`improve_pairs` with modes. Need pass `deadline`. It should not use global deadline. Implementation:
```
def improve_pairs(h, max_time, rng, modes=('gradient','adjacent','opposite','random'), search_evals=8, nu=0.02, K=None, deadline=None):
    n=h.size; S=0.5*n
    h=project_sum_box(h,S)
    c=fast_c5(h)
    if deadline is None: local_deadline = time.time() + max_time
    else: local_deadline = min(time.time()+max_time, deadline)
    pass_idx=0
    iters=0
    while time.time() < local_deadline:
       if local_deadline - time.time() < 15: break
       mode = modes[pass_idx % len(modes)]
       order = np.arange(n)
       if mode == 'random' or (mode=='gradient' and rng.random()<0.2):
           rng.shuffle(order)
       high_idx = low_idx = None; is_high = is_low = None
       if mode == 'gradient':
           g, c, _ = active_gradient(h, nu=nu, K=K, rng=rng, random_single_prob=0.0)
           gm = g - g.mean()
           if gm.size:
              q75 = float(np.quantile(gm, 0.75)); q25=float(np.quantile(gm,0.25))
              cand_high = np.where((gm > q75) & (h > 1e-6))[0]
              cand_low = np.where((gm < q25) & (h < 1.0-1e-6))[0]
              if cand_high.size and cand_low.size:
                 high_idx = cand_high; low_idx = cand_low
                 is_high = np.zeros(n,bool); is_high[cand_high]=True
                 is_low = np.zeros(n,bool); is_low[cand_low]=True
       for i in order:
           int_i = int(i)
           if mode == 'adjacent': j = (int_i + 1) % n
           elif mode == 'opposite':
               j = (int_i + n//2) % n
               if j == int_i: j = (int_i+1)%n
           elif mode == 'gradient' and is_high is not None:
               if is_high[int_i]:
                  if low_idx.size == 0: j = (int_i+1)%n
                  else:
                     j = int(low_idx[int(rng.integers(low_idx.size))])
                     if j == int_i: j=(int_i+1)%n
               elif is_low[int_i]:
                  if high_idx.size == 0: j=(int_i+1)%n
                  else:
                     j = int(high_idx[int(rng.integers(high_idx.size))])
                     if j == int_i: j=(int_i+1)%n
               else:
                  j = int(rng.integers(n)); if j==int_i: j=(int_i+1)%n
           else:
               j = int(rng.integers(n)); if j==int_i: j=(int_i+1)%n
           h,c2 = pair_minimize(h, int_i, j, c, search_evals=search_evals)
           if c2 < c - 1e-12:
               c = c2
           iters += 1
           if (iters & 15) == 0 and time.time() >= local_deadline - 15:
               break
       pass_idx +=1
    h = project_sum_box(h,S)
    return h,c
```
Potential bug: If `mode='random'`, we don't have is_high. But `np.arange` order unshuffled for adjacent/opposite. okay.

```
def resample_h(h0,n1,mode='linear'):
   if n0==n1: return np.array(h0,float,copy=True)
   if mode == 'nearest':
       idx = ((np.arange(n1)+0.5)*n0/n1).astype(np.int64)
       idx = np.clip(idx,0,n0-1)
       h1=np.empty(n1); h1[:] = h0[idx]
   else:
       x0=(np.arange(n0)+0.5)/n0
       x1=(np.arange(n1)+0.5)/n1
       h1=np.interp(x1,x0,h0,left=h0[0],right=h0[-1])
   return project_sum_box(h1,0.5*n1)
```

```
def default_start(n, kind, rng):
   x=(np.arange(n)+0.5)*2.0/n
   h=0.5*np.ones(n)
   if kind==0: h=0.5+0.5*np.cos(np.pi*x)
   elif kind==1: h=0.5+0.5*np.cos(np.pi*(x-1.0))
   else: h=0.5+0.4*np.cos(2*np.pi*x)+0.2*np.sin(2*np.pi*x)
   return project_sum_box(np.clip(h,0,1),0.5*n)
```

Let's detail `project_sum_box` efficient:
```
def project_sum_box(z,S):
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=float)
    if z.size == 0: return z
    S = float(S)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(z)):
        z = np.nan_to_num(z, nan=0.5, posinf=1.0, neginf=0.0)
    n = z.size
    if S <= 0.0: return np.zeros(n)
    if S >= float(n): return np.ones(n)
    # quick if possible
    h = z - (float(z.sum()) - S) / n
    if h.min() >= -1e-14 and h.max() <= 1.0 + 1e-14:
        h = np.clip(h,0,1)
        err = S - float(h.sum())
        if abs(err) > 1e-10:
           idx = np.where((h>1e-12)&(h<1.0-1e-12))[0]
           if idx.size: h[idx] += err/idx.size
           else: h[0] += err
        h=np.clip(h,0,1)
        # final tiny correction
        err = S - float(h.sum())
        if abs(err) > 1e-12:
           idx = np.where((h>1e-12)&(h<1.0-1e-12))[0]
           if idx.size: h[idx[0]] += err
           else:
              if err>0:
                 idx=np.where(h<1.0)[0]
                 if idx.size: h[idx[0]]=1.0
              else: idx=np.where(h>0.0)[0]; if idx.size: h[idx[0]]=0.0
        return h
    # bisection
    lo = float(np.min(z)) - 1.0
    hi = float(np.max(z)) + 1.0
    for _ in range(60):
       tau=0.5*(lo+hi)
       h=np.clip(z-tau,0,1)
       s=float(h.sum())
       if s > S: lo=tau
       else: hi=tau
    h=np.clip(z-0.5*(lo+hi),0,1)
    # residual correction similar
    ...
    return h
```
The quick branch with `h = z - mean diff` is not true projection if z out of bounds but within after? If z min<0 or max>1 but adjusted h within? possible, but if z out and h within, subtracting mean changes values; okay if within bounds. But if z min<0 and max high, h may be within, good. It is a feasible point near z? Not guaranteed nearest but fine for project exact? We want exact projection. For starts, quick might not be Euclidean; but constraints only. It is okay to use faster feasible not necessarily closest. Name project but actually feasible. Could be acceptable. But if z has extreme out bounds, quick might not feasible and bisection.

If quick branch with h within bounds but sum exact, it may be far from z if n large? It subtracts mean; okay. But not always within [0,1] if z near bounds. Bisection.

Bisection residual correction: We can reuse a helper `fix_sum(h,S)`. But no closures; define top-level `_fix_sum(h,S)`? Let's create top-level `repair_sum(h,S)` to avoid duplication.

```
def repair_sum(h, S):
    err = float(S - h.sum())
    if abs(err) <= 1e-12: return h
    idx = np.where((h > 1e-9) & (h < 1.0 - 1e-9))[0]
    if idx.size:
        h[idx] += err / idx.size
    else:
       if err > 0:
          order = np.argsort(h)
          e=err
          for idx2 in order:
              cap=1.0-h[idx2]
              if cap <=0: continue
              add = min(cap,e)
              h[idx2]+=add; e-=add
              if e <= 1e-14: break
       else:
          order = np.argsort(-h)
          e=-err
          for idx2 in order:
              sub=min(h[idx2],e)
              h[idx2]-=sub; e-=sub
              if e <=1e-14: break
    h=np.clip(h,0,1)
    err=float(S-h.sum())
    if abs(err)>1e-12:
       idx=np.where((h>1e-9)&(h<1.0-1e-9))[0]
       if idx.size: h[idx[0]] += err
       else:
          if err>0:
             idx=np.where(h<1.0)[0]
             if idx.size: h[idx[0]]=1.0
          else:
             idx=np.where(h>0.0)[0]
             if idx.size: h[idx[0]]=0.0
    return h
```
`repair_sum` modifies in place and returns h. Use in project/quick.
But if it adds err/idx.size and pushes some to bound, final clip and one-index correction. Good.

```
def quick_feasible(z,S):
    h=np.asarray(z,dtype=float).copy()
    if h.size==0: return h
    if not np.allfinite: nan_to_num
    h -= (h.sum()-S)/h.size
    h=np.clip(h,0,1)
    return repair_sum(h,S)
```

```
    h1 = resample_h(parent,n,'linear')

       c2=fast_c5(h2)
```

```
def default_start(n, kind=0, rng=None):
    x = (np.arange(n)+0.5)*2.0/n
    if kind == 0:
       h = 0.5 + 0.5*np.cos(np.pi*x)
    elif kind == 1:
       h = 0.5 + 0.5*np.cos(np.pi*(x-1.0))
    elif kind == 2:
       h = 0.5 + 0.4*np.cos(2.0*np.pi*x) + 0.25*np.sin(2.0*np.pi*x)
    else:
       h = 0.5*np.ones(n)
       if rng is not None:
           h += 0.25*rng.normal(0.0,1.0,n)
    h=np.clip(h,0,1)
    return project_sum_box(h,0.5*n)
```

### Function _term_cache

```
def _term_cache(n):
    cache = _TERM_CACHE.get(n)
    if cache is None:
        N = 1
        need = 2 * n - 1
        while N < need:
            N <<= 1
        cache = {
            'N': N,
            'd': np.arange(n, dtype=np.intp),
            'n_minus_d': np.arange(n, 0, -1, dtype=np.intp)
        }
        _TERM_CACHE[n] = cache
    return cache
```
If n=0, need=-1? We never call n=0. But if n=1, need=1, N=1, d [0], n_minus_d [1]. Good.

### compute_terms

```
def compute_terms(h):
    n = int(h.size)
    cache = _term_cache(n)
    H = np.fft.rfft(h, cache['N'])
    ac = np.fft.irfft(H * np.conj(H), cache['N'])[:n]
    pref = np.empty(n + 1, dtype=float)
    pref[0] = 0.0
    pref[1:] = np.cumsum(h, dtype=float)
    total = pref[-1]
    d = cache['d']
    idx = cache['n_minus_d']
    A = pref[idx] - ac
    B = total - pref[d] - ac
    dx = 2.0 / float(n)
    return A, B, dx
```
`pref[d]` for d array int. Good. If n large, cumsum. Good.

### fast_c5_from_terms
```
def fast_c5_from_terms(A,B,dx):
    m = float(A[0]) if A.size else 0.0
    if A.size > 1:
        ma = float(np.max(A[1:]))
        if ma > m: m=ma
        mb = float(np.max(B[1:]))
        if mb > m: m=mb
    if m < 0.0 and m > -1e-12: m=0.0
    return m * float(dx)
```
If B length n; B[1:] for n=1 empty; if A.size>1 only. Good.

### exact_c5
```
def exact_c5(h):
    n = int(h.size)
    if n == 0: return 0.0
    corr = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode='full')
    val = float(np.max(corr)) * (2.0 / float(n))
    if val < 0 and val > -1e-12: val=0.0
    return val
```

### repair_sum
Need be careful modifying idx array values then h. Use for loops.
```
def repair_sum(h, S):
    h = np.array(h, dtype=float, copy=False)  # if h not writable? It is.
```
But if `copy=False` and input not writable, error. Use `h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)`. In quick_feasible we pass copy. In project, h new. In repair, we can modify in place. Use `h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)`.
```
    err = float(S - h.sum())
    if abs(err) <= 1e-12: return h
    idx = np.where((h > 1e-9) & (h < 1.0 - 1e-9))[0]
    if idx.size > 0:
        h[idx] += err / float(idx.size)
    else:
        if err > 0:
            order = np.argsort(h)
            rem = err
            for k in order:
                cap = 1.0 - h[k]
                if cap <= 0.0: continue
                add = cap if cap < rem else rem
                h[k] += add
                rem -= add
                if rem <= 1e-14: break
        else:
            order = np.argsort(-h)
            rem = -err
            for k in order:
                sub = h[k] if h[k] < rem else rem
                h[k] -= sub
                rem -= sub
                if rem <= 1e-14: break
    h = np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0)
    err = float(S - h.sum())
    if abs(err) > 1e-13:
        idx = np.where((h > 1e-9) & (h < 1.0 - 1e-9))[0]
        if idx.size > 0:
            h[idx[0]] += err
        else:
            if err > 0:
                idx = np.where(h < 1.0)[0]
                if idx.size > 0: h[idx[0]] = 1.0
            else:
                idx = np.where(h > 0.0)[0]
                if idx.size > 0: h[idx[0]] = 0.0
    return h
```
If after equal distribute, some h clipped, sum change; second correction. If second correction pushes one out, we don't clip again; but err small. Could after second set, if out of bounds? If h idx near bound and err large? First equal distribute might clip; second err could be larger but still small? In bisection, err after 60 bisections tiny (<1e-10). In quick, after mean+clip maybe err maybe not tiny if many clipped; equal distribute can clip some, second one-index may push out. But if err large, equal distribute with many nonbounds could not be enough? For worst z all 0 except need S=100, h after mean? Quick: z maybe all 0, h -= -S/n = +S/n (e.g., 0.5), clip, sum exact. Okay. If z all 2, quick h -= (2n-S)/n => all 1.5? clip 1, sum n, err S-n negative large; equal distribute over nonbounds none; order argsort(-h) all 1, sub rem up to S? It will subtract from many, okay. h final maybe 0/1 sum exact. Good.
But `repair_sum` after sequential if err large changes sum but may leave h nonbounds? okay.

If h is a view of z? In quick, copy. In project quick branch, h new. In bisection, h new. Good.

### project_sum_box

```
if z.size == 0: return z
S = float(S)
if not np.all(np.isfinite(z)):
```

`quick_feasible`:
```
def quick_feasible(z,S):
    h = np.array(z, dtype=float, copy=True)
    if h.size ==0: return h
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(h)): h=np.nan_to_num(...)
    h -= (float(h.sum()) - float(S)) / float(h.size)
    h = np.clip(h,0,1)
    return repair_sum(h,S)
```

### smooth_h
Manual:
```
def smooth_h(h, sigma):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float)
    if sigma <= 0 or h.size ==0: return h.copy()
    r = int(math.ceil(3.0 * float(sigma)))
    if r <=0: return h.copy()
    x = np.arange(-r, r+1, dtype=float)
    k = np.exp(-0.5 * (x / float(sigma)) ** 2)
    total = k.sum()
    if total == 0: return h.copy()
    k /= total
    hp = np.pad(h, r, mode='edge')
    out = np.convolve(hp, k, mode='same')[:h.size]
    return out
```
Need verify take first h.size. For even r? kernel symmetric. Good.

### resample_h
```
def resample_h(h0,n1,mode='linear'):
    h0=np.asarray(h0,dtype=float)
    n0=h0.size
    if n1 <=0: return np.array([])
    if n0==1:
       return np.full(n1, h0[0])
    if n0==n1:
       return h0.copy()
    if mode == 'nearest':
       frac = (np.arange(n1) + 0.5) * (float(n0)/float(n1))
       idx = np.floor(frac).astype(np.intp)
       idx = np.clip(idx, 0, n0-1)
       h1 = h0[idx]
    else:
       x0 = (np.arange(n0)+0.5)/float(n0)
       x1 = (np.arange(n1)+0.5)/float(n1)
       h1 = np.interp(x1, x0, h0, left=h0[0], right=h0[-1])
    h1 = np.asarray(h1,dtype=float)
    return project_sum_box(h1, 0.5*float(n1))
```
For nearest, floor of center index may produce no last? okay.

### make_best_start
```
def make_best_start(parent, n, rng=None):
    h = resample_h(parent, n, 'linear')
    c = fast_c5(h)
    best = h
    if n > parent.size:
       h2 = resample_h(parent,n,'nearest')
       c2 = fast_c5(h2)
       if c2 < c:
          best=h2; c=c2
       sigma = max(0.5, 1.0*math.sqrt(float(n)/float(parent.size)))
       h3 = smooth_h(best, sigma)
       h3 = project_sum_box(h3, 0.5*n)
       c3=fast_c5(h3)
       if c3 < c:
          best=h3; c=c3
       if rng is not None:
          h4 = quick_feasible(best + rng.normal(0.0, 0.05, n), 0.5*n)
          c4=fast_c5(h4)
          if c4 < c: best=h4; c=c4
    elif rng is not None and n > 16:
       h4 = quick_feasible(best + rng.normal(0.0,0.02,n),0.5*n)
       c4=fast_c5(h4)
       if c4 < c: best=h4
    return best
```
If no parent and no rng, okay. Jitter only if improves fast. But if jitter worse, not used. Could use a different start even if worse, but no.

### default_start
Could include random. `rng` optional.
```
def default_start(n, kind=0, rng=None):
    x = (np.arange(n)+0.5)*2.0/float(n)
    if kind == 0:
       h = 0.5 + 0.5*np.cos(np.pi*x)
    elif kind == 1:
       h = 0.5 + 0.5*np.cos(np.pi*(x-1.0))
    elif kind == 2:
       h = 0.5 + 0.4*np.cos(2.0*np.pi*x) + 0.2*np.sin(2.0*np.pi*x)
    elif kind == 3:
       h = 0.5 + 0.4*np.cos(np.pi*x) + 0.3*np.sin(2.0*np.pi*x)
    else:
       h = 0.5*np.ones(n)
       if rng is not None:
          h = h + 0.2*rng.normal(0.0,1.0,n)
    return project_sum_box(np.clip(h,0,1), 0.5*n)
```

### gradient_from_weights
```
def gradient_from_weights(h,wA,wB,dx):
    n=h.size
    g=np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    nzA = np.nonzero(wA > 0.0)[0]
    for d in nzA:
       ww = float(wA[d])
       if d == 0:
          g += ww * (1.0 - 2.0*h)
       else:
          mp = n - int(d)
          g[:mp] += ww * (1.0 - h[d:])
          g[d:] -= ww * h[:-d]
    nzB = np.nonzero(wB > 0.0)[0]
    for d in nzB:
       ww = float(wB[d])
       if d == 0:
          g += ww * (1.0 - 2.0*h)
       else:
          g[d:] += ww * (1.0 - h[:-d])
          g[:n-int(d)] -= ww * h[d:]
    g *= float(dx)
    return g
```
`np.nonzero(wA > 0.0)` fine.

### improve_pg
```
def improve_pg(h,max_time=60.0,rng=None,nu=0.04,K=None,initial_step=0.10,deadline=None):
    h=project_sum_box(h,0.5*len(h))
    n=h.size; S=0.5*float(n)
    c=fast_c5(h)
    if max_time <=0 or n<2: return h,c
    now=time.time()
    if deadline is None: ld=now+max_time
    else: ld=min(now+max_time, float(deadline))
    step=float(initial_step)
    stagnant=0
    iter_count=0
    while time.time() < ld:
       if ld - time.time() < 10: break
       iter_count +=1
       g,c_new,_ = active_gradient(h, nu=nu, K=K, rng=rng, random_single_prob=0.25)
       if c_new < c - 1e-12: c=c_new
       max_abs=float(np.max(np.abs(g))) if g.size else 0.0
       if max_abs < 1e-14:
          stagnant += 3
       else:
          direction = -g / max_abs
          t=step
          accepted=False
          best_try=None
          for _ in range(10):
             if time.time() >= ld: break
             cand = quick_feasible(h + t * direction, S)
             ctry = fast_c5(cand)
             if ctry < c - 1e-12:
                best_try=cand; accepted=True; c=ctry; break
             t *= 0.5
             if t < 1e-5: break
          if accepted:
             h = best_try
             stagnant = 0
             step = min(0.35, max(0.02, t * 1.5 + 0.01))
          else:
             stagnant += 1
             step = max(0.005, step * 0.6)
       if iter_count % 150 == 0:
          nu = max(0.004, nu * 0.8)
       if stagnant > 20:
          break
    h = project_sum_box(h,S)
    return h,c
```
If active_gradient returns c_new slightly higher due recompute? It shouldn't. If `h` changed? same. Use c_new always maybe: `c = c_new` to keep fast current. But if c_new > c due numerical, we might think no improvement? Use `if c_new < c: c = c_new else: c = min(c, c_new)`. Simpler set `c = c_new` because h same; any difference numerical. But if h from previous had full sum? same. Use `c = c_new`.
In line search, if `h + t*direction` may have nan? no.
If `quick_feasible` expensive. okay.

### _pair_eval
```
def _pair_eval(h,i,j,a,b,t):
    x = a + t; y = b - t
    if x < 0.0: x = 0.0
    elif x > 1.0: x = 1.0
    if y < 0.0: y = 0.0
    elif y > 1.0: y = 1.0
    old_i = h[i]; old_j = h[j]
    h[i] = x; h[j] = y
    c = fast_c5(h)
    h[i] = old_i; h[j] = old_j
    return c
```
`old_i` should equal a,b but use old.

### pair_minimize
Detailed:
```
def pair_minimize(h,i,j,c_current,search_evals=8,tol=1e-12):
    if i == j: return h,c_current
    a = float(h[i]); b = float(h[j])
    lo = max(-a, b - 1.0)
    hi = min(1.0 - a, b)
    if lo > 0.0: lo = 0.0
    if hi < 0.0: hi = 0.0
    span = hi - lo
    if span <= 1e-12: return h,c_current
    eps = 1e-3 * span
    if eps < 1e-6: eps = 1e-6
    if eps > 1e-2: eps = 1e-2
    best_t = 0.0; best_f = float(c_current)
    pos_improved = False; neg_improved = False
    tpos = hi
    if eps < hi: tpos = eps
    if tpos > lo + 1e-12:
       f = _pair_eval(h,i,j,a,b,tpos)
       if f < best_f - tol:
          best_f = f; best_t = tpos; pos_improved = True
    tneg = lo
    if -eps > lo: tneg = -eps
    if tneg < hi - 1e-12:
       f = _pair_eval(h,i,j,a,b,tneg)
       if f < best_f - tol:
          best_f = f; best_t = tneg; neg_improved = True
    if best_t == 0.0:
       return h,c_current
    lo_g = lo; hi_g = hi
    if pos_improved and not neg_improved:
       lo_g = 0.0
    elif neg_improved and not pos_improved:
       hi_g = 0.0
    if hi_g <= lo_g + 1e-12:
       # only small improvement known? Accept if best_t within
       if best_f < c_current - tol:
          h[i] = a + best_t; h[j] = b - best_t
          return h,best_f
       return h,c_current
    c1 = lo_g + _GOLDGR * (hi_g - lo_g)
    c2 = hi_g - _GOLDGR * (hi_g - lo_g)
    f1 = _pair_eval(...c1); if f1<best_f: ...
    f2 = _pair_eval(...c2); ...
    for _ in range(max(0,int(search_evals))):
       if f1 < f2:
          hi_g = c2
          c2 = c1; f2 = f1
          c1 = lo_g + _GOLDGR * (hi_g - lo_g)
          f1 = _pair_eval(...)
          if f1 < best_f: best_f=f1; best_t=c1
       else:
          lo_g = c1
          c1 = c2; f1 = f2
          c2 = hi_g - _GOLDGR * (hi_g - lo_g)
          f2 = _pair_eval(...)
          if f2 < best_f: best_f=f2; best_t=c2
       if hi_g - lo_g <= 1e-10 * max(1.0, abs(hi_g), abs(lo_g)):
          break
    # check midpoint and endpoints
    for ttest in (0.5*(lo_g+hi_g), lo_g, hi_g):
       if ttest < lo_g - 1e-12 or ttest > hi_g + 1e-12: continue
       if abs(ttest) < 1e-14 and best_t == 0.0: pass? current known.
       f = _pair_eval(...ttest)
       if f < best_f: best_f=f; best_t=ttest
    if best_f < float(c_current) - tol and best_t != 0.0:
       h[i] = a + best_t; h[j] = b - best_t
       return h,best_f
    return h,c_current
```
If best_t from small check and golden/cendpoint not lower, accept small. Good.
But if best_t = 0? no. If best_t = -0? okay.
If pos_improved true, lo_g=0; but if best_t positive and hi=0? pos not possible. If small tpos improved near 0, golden [0,hi]. Good.
If both small improved (rare), full lo/hi. If pos false but best_f improved? no.
Endpoint evaluation may evaluate t=0 (known) if lo=0; could waste one eval. We can skip if abs(t)<1e-14. Good.

### improve_pairs
Need to use `active_gradient` and rely on rng. If rng None? In run always. But if None, create default? Could inside if rng None: rng=np.random.default_rng(0). Good.
```
def improve_pairs(h,max_time=60.0,rng=None,modes=('gradient','adjacent','opposite','random'),search_evals=8,nu=0.02,K=None,deadline=None):
    if rng is None: rng = np.random.default_rng(12345)
    h=project_sum_box(h,0.5*len(h))
    n=h.size; S=0.5*float(n)
    c=fast_c5(h)
    if max_time <=0 or n<2: return h,c
    now=time.time(); ld=...
    pass_idx=0; iters=0
    while time.time() < ld:
       if ld - time.time() < 10: break
       mode = modes[pass_idx % len(modes)]
       order = np.arange(n)
       if mode == 'random':
           rng.shuffle(order)
       elif mode == 'gradient' and rng.random() < 0.25:
           rng.shuffle(order)
       use_grad = False
       is_high = None; is_low=None; high_idx=None; low_idx=None
       if mode == 'gradient':
           g,c_new,_ = active_gradient(h, nu=nu, K=K, rng=rng, random_single_prob=0.0)
           c = c_new
           gm = g - float(g.mean())
           if n > 4:
              q75 = float(np.quantile(gm, 0.75)); q25 = float(np.quantile(gm, 0.25))
              high_idx = np.where((gm > q75) & (h > 1e-6))[0]
              low_idx = np.where((gm < q25) & (h < 1.0 - 1e-6))[0]
              if high_idx.size > 0 and low_idx.size > 0:
                  use_grad = True
                  is_high = np.zeros(n, dtype=bool); is_high[high_idx]=True
                  is_low = np.zeros(n, dtype=bool); is_low[low_idx]=True
       for i in order:
          if ld - time.time() < 8: break
          ii = int(i)
          if mode == 'adjacent': jj=(ii+1)%n
          elif mode == 'opposite': jj=(ii + n//2) % n; if jj==ii: jj=(ii+1)%n
          elif use_grad:
             if is_high[ii]:
                if low_idx.size == 0: jj=(ii+1)%n
                else: jj=int(low_idx[int(rng.integers(low_idx.size))]); if jj==ii: jj=(ii+1)%n
             elif is_low[ii]:
                if high_idx.size ==0: jj=(ii+1)%n
                else: jj=int(high_idx[int(rng.integers(high_idx.size))]); if jj==ii: jj=(ii+1)%n
             else:
                jj=int(rng.integers(n)); if jj==ii: jj=(ii+1)%n
          else:
             jj=int(rng.integers(n)); if jj==ii: jj=(ii+1)%n
          if jj == ii: continue
          h,c2 = pair_minimize(h, ii, jj, c, search_evals=search_evals)
          if c2 < c - 1e-12:
             c = c2
          iters += 1
          if (iters & 31) == 0 and ld - time.time() < 12:
             break
       pass_idx += 1
    h = project_sum_box(h,S)
    return h,c
```
If mode random, `use_grad` false and jj random. If mode gradient but use_grad false, jj random. Good. But for gradient mode, if order not shuffled, pairs maybe. ok.

Potential performance: `rng.integers` returns Python int? int(). Good.

Could pair update same unordered pair twice in opposite pass (i and i+n/2) with order all i. That's fine but doubles effort. We could only i<n//2 for opposite, but then pass length less. Fine.

### run details

```
def run(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    t0=time.time()
    budget = max(1.0, float(budget_s))
    if budget > 60.0:
       reserve = min(30.0, max(5.0, 0.02*budget))
    else:
       reserve = max(0.5, 0.1*budget)
    final_deadline = t0 + budget - reserve
    if final_deadline < t0 + 1.0:
       final_deadline = t0 + max(1.0, budget*0.9)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    best_h=None; best_c=float('inf'); best_n=0
    pv = globals().get('initial_h_values', None)
    if pv is not None:
       try:
          p = np.asarray(pv, dtype=float).ravel()
       except Exception:
          p = None
       if p is not None and p.size >= 4 and np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
          p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1.0)
          if p.size % 2 != 0:
             p_even = resample_h(p, p.size + 1, 'nearest')
          else:
             p_even = project_sum_box(p, 0.5*float(p.size))
          parent = p_even
       else:
          parent = None
    else: parent=None
    if parent is not None:
       cpar = exact_c5(parent)
       best_h = parent.copy(); best_c = cpar; best_n = parent.size
    else:
       parent = None
    # targets
    starts=[]
    if parent is not None:
       base = parent.size
       if base < 4: base=64
       if base %2 !=0: base +=1
       if base < 16: base=16
       if budget >= 700.0:
          max_target = 512
       elif budget >= 350.0:
          max_target = 320
       elif budget >= 150.0:
          max_target = 160
       else:
          max_target = base
       max_target = max(max_target, base)
       cand_targets = [base]
       for t in (2*base, 4*base, 8*base):
          if t <= max_target and t not in cand_targets:
             cand_targets.append(t)
       if 512 <= max_target and 512 not in cand_targets:
          cand_targets.append(512)
       # maybe if max_target>512 and base< max, include max_target
       if max_target not in cand_targets and max_target > 512:
          cand_targets.append(max_target)
       seen=set()
       for t in sorted(set(cand_targets)):
          if t < 4: continue
          if t%2 !=0: t +=1
          if t in seen: continue
          seen.add(t)
          if t != parent.size:
             hstart = make_best_start(parent, t, rng)
          else:
             hstart = project_sum_box(parent, 0.5*float(t))
          starts.append(hstart)
    else:
       if budget >= 700: tlist=[128,256,512]
       elif budget >= 350: tlist=[128,256]
       elif budget >= 120: tlist=[128]
       else: tlist=[64]
       for t in tlist:
          best=None; bc=inf
          for kind in range(5):
             h0=default_start(t, kind, rng)
             hc=fast_c5(h0)
             if hc<bc: best=h0; bc=hc
          if best is not None: starts.append(best)
    if not starts:
       n = 64 if budget < 120 else 128
       h = default_start(n, 0, rng)
       starts.append(h)
    # optimize
    if time.time() < final_deadline - 10:
       total_opt = max(1.0, final_deadline - time.time())
       weights = [1.0,1.7,2.3,3.0,3.5][:len(starts)]
       sw = float(sum(weights))
       for idx,h0 in enumerate(starts):
          if time.time() >= final_deadline - 15: break
          n0 = h0.size
          alloc = (weights[idx]/sw) * total_opt
          # if previous took extra, remaining may less than alloc? allocate but functions cap; use if alloc<=5 skip?
          if alloc < 10.0:
             # just exact eval
             ce=exact_c5(project_sum_box(h0,0.5*n0)); update
             continue
          # PG
          pg_frac = 0.40 if n0 <=160 else (0.30 if n0 <=320 else 0.20)
          pg_time = alloc * pg_frac
          if pg_time > 20.0:
             hopt, copt = improve_pg(h0, max_time=pg_time, rng=rng, nu=0.04, deadline=min(final_deadline, time.time()+alloc-5))
          else:
             hopt, copt = h0, fast_c5(h0)
          # update best after PG maybe
          if copt < best_c + 1e-5:
              ce = exact_c5(hopt)
              if ce < best_c: best...
          pair_time = max(1.0, alloc - pg_time - 5.0)
          if time.time() < final_deadline - 10:
             hopt, copt = improve_pairs(hopt, max_time=pair_time, rng=rng, deadline=min(final_deadline, time.time()+pair_time))
          # update best
          if copt < best_c + 1e-5:
              ce=exact_c5(hopt)
              if ce < best_c: best...
    # final exact
    if best_h is None:
       n=128; h=default_start(n,0,rng); best_h=h; best_c=exact_c5(h); best_n=n
    else:
       best_h = np.array(best_h, dtype=float, copy=True)
       best_h = project_sum_box(best_h, 0.5*float(best_h.size))
       best_c = exact_c5(best_h)
       best_n = int(best_h.size)
    return best_h, float(best_c), best_n
```
